## v0.4 True Threaded Chat State

- Scope: fake-only multi-turn threaded chat replacing the remaining single-answer query flow.
- Proof directory: `/tmp/proof_kia_stick_v04_true_threaded_chat_20260620T145019Z`
- Manual QA: PASS by headless Chrome/CDP multi-turn smoke on `127.0.0.1:3011`; port `3010` was already in use.
- Thread model: explicit `ConversationThread`, `ChatMessage`, `UserMessage`, and `AssistantMessage` records with stable IDs.
- Chat flow: user and assistant messages append chronologically; prior turns remain visible.
- Composer: starts empty, uses `Message KIA Stick...`, primary action says `Send`, blank send is blocked, Enter sends, Shift+Enter inserts newline, and the draft clears immediately after send.
- Persistence: current fake thread restores from local browser storage under a separate key from Saved Answers.
- Controls: `New chat` confirms before clearing non-empty threads.
- Assistant messages: each response owns Save, full-packet expansion, and citation expansion state.
- Loading/failure: submitted user message stays visible with a temporary assistant row and retry support on failure.
- Governor context: recent fake-thread history is passed into `buildAnswer`; evidence, verbal denial, supervisor wording, and next-step follow-ups resolve against prior topic.
- Clarification: unresolved follow-ups ask for the topic instead of inventing context.
- Real/private document access: none.
- Push performed: no.

## Public data pilot 1A public Chat routing repair

- Phase: `KIA-Stick-public-data-pilot-1A-public-chat-routing-repair`.
- Base implementation commit: `7995d9802f1710163b443fafcb7dbe7aa723e06e`; accepted pushed baseline remains `v1.1.77 at 80e53c7` / `80e53c74ea86c2e83c797011728efc138b800f0e`.
- Proven root cause: Chat defaulted to a hidden fake source selector and branched before any public-intent classification, so the valid public cache and public governor were unreachable from the normal composer path.
- Repair: visible automatic/fake/public lane policy; automatic routing for the bounded NLRB demonstrations; shared submit router; public/fake card identity; exact public citation navigation; explicit Saved public metadata; public no-answer isolation; component-path regression coverage.
- Validation commands: focused public Chat routing tests; `npm run lint`; `npm run typecheck`; `env -u KIA_PROOF_ROOT npm test -- --testTimeout 20000`; `npm run build`; `npm run design:check`; `npm run release:check`; `npm run governance:report`; `npm run queue:next`; `npm run operator:smoke`; `PROOF_DIR=... npm run qa`; `npm run scan:fake`; `npm run scan:privacy`; package/lock immutability checks; `git diff --check`; in-app browser execution of all four exact questions, citation jump, Saved public record, and fake/public separation.
- Files changed: `app/globals.css`, `components/KiaStickApp.tsx`, `lib/answerGovernor.ts`, `lib/chatAnswerRouter.ts`, `lib/conversationModel.ts`, `lib/publicSource.ts`, `lib/publicSourceAnswer.ts`, `lib/savedAnswers.ts`, `tests/publicChatRoutingIntegration.test.ts`, and this progress entry.
- Proof: `/home/mint/kia-stick-local-proofs/proof_kia_stick_public_data_pilot_1A_public_chat_routing_repair_20260717T113055Z`.
- Safety: one existing allowlisted NLRB source only; no private data, additional URL, CBA fetch, upload, file reader, arbitrary path, OCR, embedding, vector store, external AI, dependency change, service change, notification, Discord, or push.
- Operator QA: PASS by `OPERATOR_QA_PASS for /home/mint/kia-stick-local-proofs/proof_kia_stick_public_data_pilot_1A_public_chat_routing_repair_20260717T113055Z`; recording proof is `/home/mint/kia-stick-local-proofs/proof_kia_stick_public_data_pilot_1A_public_chat_routing_repair_operator_qa_pass_recording_20260717T121743Z`.

## Public Steward Workflow Platform Bundle 2 employee-claims copy repair

- Phase: `KIA-Stick-public-steward-workflow-platform-bundle-2-employee-claims-copy-repair`.
- Baseline: clean local Bundle 2 at `21b66aa7948468a3991d82a2012f479a7d72fd33`; accepted capability remains Bundle 1 and Discipline Copy Repair at `ea0ce8de9cd6b85b56528fabc9e8ca7f8bf43a52`; remote `main` remains `f86475f388cb8e01cc2e516fb9f5ee7a13ef7e6c`.

## Sensitive-Data Gate 0B — disabled-account closure authority revision (S1) — 2026-09-15

- Phase: `KIA-Stick-sensitive-data-gate-0B-disabled-account-closure-authority-revision`. Bounded single-finding revision of `docs/sensitive-data-gate-0b-auth-design.md` (revision 3 commit `297f7343d6d3c1a2c61f7e767e9b1663cf32e24d`) resolving the one remaining material re-QA ambiguity `S1_DISABLED_CLOSURE_AUTHORITY_UNRECONCILED`. Design/security-architecture only; no auth, authorization, closure, deletion or encryption implemented, and Gate 0C not started.
- The contradiction: §17.1 promised a `DISABLED` → `CLOSED` election, §4.6 required session-bound re-authentication for account closure, and `DISABLED_ORDINARY_SESSION_AUTHORITY=invalid` — so no authority for disabled closure was ever defined.
- Classification determined independently, not assumed: `DISABLED_CLOSURE_POLICY_CLASSIFICATION=D6_SEPARATE_OWNER_DECISION`. Applying the same independence test that separated D5 from D4 (written out in the new §17.3), holding D5 at either outcome leaves the owner a real choice about disabled self-closure, and the security profiles differ in kind — reactivation is restorative, closure is irreversible and destructive. Owner-decision count moved 5 → 6; the previous count was not preserved.
- Changes: §4.6 and §4.9 separate `ACTIVE_ACCOUNT_CLOSURE_AUTHORITY` from `DISABLED_ACCOUNT_CLOSURE_AUTHORITY`; §9.4 adds `OPERATOR_ACCOUNT_CLOSURE_POWER=none` / `APPLICATION_ADMIN_CLOSURE_POWER=none`; §17.1 specifies a bounded `ACCOUNT_CLOSURE_CEREMONY` (recovery-authority entry only, `ACCOUNT_CLOSURE_CEREMONY_PRIVATE_DATA_ACCESS=deny`, single success `DISABLED` → `CLOSED`, authority ends at transition) and states that closure is not private-data deletion or key destruction; new §17.3 registers D6 and old §17.3 renumbered to §17.4; §20/§21 (THREAT-020/021/022)/§22 (new tests 40-44)/§23/§25/§26 updated consistently, with §26.6 carrying the full D6 register entry and §26.5(a)'s hidden closure assumption withdrawn.
- Preserved: `CLOSED_REACTIVATION_ALLOWED=no`, `CLOSED_STATE_TERMINAL_FOR_GATE_0B=yes`, D1-D5, D3 Gate 0C boundary, `PRIVATE_DATA_RECOVERABILITY_AFTER_AUTH_RECOVERY=UNRESOLVED_PENDING_GATE_0C`, `SESSION_AUDIT_ID` separation, no operator recovery/reset/reactivation, MFA qualification, browser/TLS model, and all non-authorization gates. `OWNER_DECISIONS_ACCEPTED=0`; no implementation default for D6.

## Sensitive-Data Gate 0B — D6 independence rationale reconciliation (W1) — 2026-09-15

- Phase: `KIA-Stick-sensitive-data-gate-0B-D6-rationale-reconciliation`. Exactly one bounded documentation revision of `docs/sensitive-data-gate-0b-auth-design.md` (revision 4 commit `843f005f0c09d347b354996308f087ff8d463e09`) resolving the single remaining WARN finding `W1_D5_D6_COMPARATIVE_HARM_OVERSTATEMENT` from the final independent GPT/Codex re-QA. Design/planning only; no auth, authorization, closure, deletion, encryption or private-data functionality implemented, scaffolded or authorized, and Gate 0C not started.
- Baseline verified exactly as expected: branch `omarchy-path-setup`, `HEAD=843f005f0c09d347b354996308f087ff8d463e09`, parent `297f7343d6d3c1a2c61f7e767e9b1663cf32e24d`, `origin/main` = live remote `main` = `52dd6bcb87e63528156df4eb7786ba98e4cd0a78`, 4 ahead / 0 behind, worktree clean, `PRODUCT_VERSION=0.7.0`. The three `/home/slimy` global bootstrap files remain absent; repo-local bootstrap used. `GLOBAL_BOOTSTRAP_FILES_PRESENT=no`.
- WARN proof verified before any edit: `/home/slimy/.local/state/kia-stick/proofs/proof_kia_stick_sensitive_data_gate_0b_disabled_closure_final_independent_reqa_20260915T170618Z`, `sha256sum -c SHA256SUMS` all OK with zero failures, `RESULT=WARN`, single finding W1 recovered verbatim from `findings.md` rather than from the task paraphrase. W1's evidence names design lines 1016, 1453 and 1455.
- The finding, restated from its own wording: D6 is defensible as a distinct owner decision, but revision 4 justified it by claiming D6's harm is strictly greater than D5's and that D6 uniquely permits permanent closure. That is unsupported, because under D5(b) whoever completes the reactivation ceremony reaches `ACTIVE`, §17.1 applies the §11.2 lifecycle in full to the consumed material, that lifecycle mandates issuance of replacement recovery material, the §11.1/§11.2 credential-reset ceremony is then reachable, a fresh ordinary authentication can follow, and closure is available under `ACTIVE_ACCOUNT_CLOSURE_AUTHORITY`. So the D5 path can also culminate in a permanently closed account.
- Independence re-tested rather than assumed, and it still holds, so the classification is preserved: `DISABLED_CLOSURE_POLICY_CLASSIFICATION=D6_SEPARATE_OWNER_DECISION`; `D6_CLASSIFICATION_DEFENSIBLE=yes`. The retained justification is purely logical independence plus a difference in authority surface — D5 asks whether the accepted recovery authority may run a limited reactivation ceremony back to `ACTIVE`; D6 asks whether it may run a limited closure ceremony directly to `CLOSED` **while the account remains `DISABLED`, without first restoring ordinary account authority**. Each is settable at either value without deciding the other.
- Claims withdrawn, not softened: `D6_HARM_DOMINANCE_CLAIM_REMOVED=yes`; `D6_IS_ONLY_PATH_TO_PERMANENT_ACCOUNT_CLOSURE=no`; `D6_STRICTLY_GREATER_HARM_THAN_D5=no`; `D5_CAN_EVENTUALLY_LEAD_TO_ACTIVE_CLOSURE_ACKNOWLEDGED=yes`. The earlier "decisive asymmetry", "terminal denial-of-access capability that D5(b) does not give them", "the worst case is an unwanted reactivation" and "the cost of getting (b) wrong is strictly higher than the cost of getting D5(b) wrong" statements are gone; what remains is a directness statement (one ceremony, no intermediate `ACTIVE` state, no replacement credential, no fresh ordinary authentication, no interval in which the true owner could observe the account alive again), explicitly labelled as not a harm ordering. §26.5's D5 tradeoffs now also state plainly that an unwanted reactivation is **not** D5(b)'s worst case, correcting the understated decision material W1 flagged.
- Added to §17.3: an explicit four-row D5/D6 independence matrix covering (a,a) no outgoing transition, (b,a) indirect closure via reactivation → fresh login → ordinary `ACTIVE` closure, (a,b) direct closure only with no return to `ACTIVE`, and (b,b) both routes with the owner choosing. Rows two and three both end in a closed account by different sequences, which is exactly why the classification rests on independence rather than comparative harm. `D5_D6_INDEPENDENCE_MATRIX_PRESENT=yes`; `D5_D6_LOGICAL_INDEPENDENCE=PASS`. ## Sensitive-Data Gate 0B — recovery-credential terminal lifecycle reconciliation (W2) — 2026-09-15

- Phase: `KIA-Stick-sensitive-data-gate-0B-recovery-credential-terminal-lifecycle-reconciliation`. Exactly one bounded documentation revision of `docs/sensitive-data-gate-0b-auth-design.md` (revision 5 commit `bc7de954944771839df61d343d8dc8aeb0ddf497`) resolving the single remaining WARN finding `W2_CLOSURE_REPLACEMENT_LIFECYCLE_CONTRADICTION` from the final independent GPT/Codex re-QA. Design/planning only; no authentication, authorization, account closure, deletion, encryption or private-data functionality implemented, scaffolded or authorized, and Gate 0C not started.
- Baseline verified exactly as expected before any edit: branch `omarchy-path-setup`, `HEAD=bc7de954944771839df61d343d8dc8aeb0ddf497`, parent `843f005f0c09d347b354996308f087ff8d463e09`, `origin/main` = live remote `main` = `52dd6bcb87e63528156df4eb7786ba98e4cd0a78`, 5 ahead / 0 behind, worktree clean, `PRODUCT_VERSION=0.7.0`. - WARN proof verified before any edit: `/home/slimy/.local/state/kia-stick/proofs/proof_kia_stick_sensitive_data_gate_0b_d6_rationale_final_independent_reqa_20260915T205005Z`, `sha256sum -c SHA256SUMS` 48/48 OK with zero failures, `RESULT=WARN`, single remaining finding W2 recovered verbatim from `findings.md` rather than from the task paraphrase. W2's evidence names design lines 661 (§11.2), 976 and 1018 (§17.1/§17.3) and 1025/1473 (§17.3/§26.6). `LATEST_WARN_PROOF_VERIFIED=PASS`.
- The contradiction, restated from its own wording: §11.2's "Use forces replacement" bullet made replacement-on-use **unconditional**; §17.1's `ACCOUNT_CLOSURE_CEREMONY` applies the §11.2 lifecycle "in full"; yet §17.3 line 1025 and §26.6 line 1473 state affirmatively that a direct `DISABLED` → `CLOSED` closure issues no replacement credential. For the D6(b) case all three cannot serve as one unambiguous specification, and the same "in full" wording is load-bearing at line 1018 for the D5 replacement claim, so treating it as inapplicable for D6 would have silently invented an exception.
- Repair: the §11.2 consumption rule is now **outcome-aware** rather than exception-based. A successful use always and permanently invalidates the credential under every outcome; what it *obliges* afterwards follows from the state the ceremony leaves the account in. `RECOVERY_LIFECYCLE_OUTCOME_AWARE=yes`; `RECOVERY_CREDENTIAL_USED_FOR_NONTERMINAL_RECOVERY=CONSUME_AND_REPLACE_OR_ROTATE`; `RECOVERY_CREDENTIAL_USED_FOR_TERMINAL_CLOSURE=CONSUME_WITHOUT_REPLACEMENT`; `W2_RECOVERY_REPLACEMENT_CONTRADICTION_RESOLVED=yes`.
- Nonterminal branch (ordinary credential reset §11.1, or `DISABLED` → `ACTIVE` reactivation under D5(b)): consumption **mandates** confirmed replacement issuance; continuing recovery capability is not treated as available until that issuance is confirmed; a failed or unconfirmed replacement still leaves the defined state with the outstanding obligation recorded and surfaced; the replacement is never an ordinary login credential. `NONTERMINAL_RECOVERY_REPLACEMENT_REQUIRED=yes`.
- Terminal branch (the `DISABLED` → `CLOSED` transition of §17.1's `ACCOUNT_CLOSURE_CEREMONY`, if D6 is ever answered (b)): the credential is consumed and permanently invalidated, **no replacement recovery credential is issued**, no outstanding replacement obligation arises or is recorded, no ordinary session is created, and no new authentication or recovery authority of any kind is created — because `CLOSED` is terminal and has no continuing authority for a replacement to serve, and issuing one would manufacture exactly the residual access authority closure exists to end. `TERMINAL_CLOSURE_REPLACEMENT_ISSUED=no`.
- Explicitly **not** weakened, and stated as such in the document: single-use semantics, replay prevention (`RECOVERY_CREDENTIAL_REPLAY_AFTER_USE_ALLOWED=no` under every outcome), predecessor invalidation, `CONCURRENTLY_VALID_RECOVERY_CREDENTIALS=at_most_one`, confirmed issuance, content-free audit, no plaintext logging, and no operator recovery authority. A third bullet fixes the edge the rule could otherwise blur: a closure ceremony that fails, is abandoned or expires reaches no terminal state, so the nonterminal rule and its replacement obligation apply — terminality is conditioned on the transition having actually occurred, never on the ceremony having been entered.
- Consume-without-replacement is bounded to the credential lifecycle and to nothing else. It deletes no private content, destroys no keys, performs no cryptographic erasure or backup eradication, and touches nothing Gate 0C owns. `ACCOUNT_CLOSURE_IMPLIES_PRIVATE_DATA_DELETION=no`; `ACCOUNT_CLOSURE_IMPLIES_KEY_DESTRUCTION=no`; `ACCOUNT_CLOSURE_IMPLIES_CRYPTOGRAPHIC_ERASURE=no`; `ACCOUNT_CLOSURE_IMPLIES_BACKUP_ERADICATION=no`; `PRIVATE_DATA_DISPOSITION_AFTER_ACCOUNT_CLOSURE=UNRESOLVED_PENDING_LATER_GATES`.
- **No new owner decision was created.** The terminal/nonterminal distinction is a lifecycle consequence of the resulting account state, not a separate product policy, and the repository produced no evidence that the owner has a real choice here: under D6(b) issuing a replacement for a terminally closed account would contradict `CLOSED_STATE_TERMINAL_FOR_GATE_0B=yes` and `CLOSED_REACTIVATION_ALLOWED=no`, which are already-settled invariants rather than open questions, so there is no coherent alternative outcome to register. `OWNER_DECISION_COUNT=6`; `OWNER_DECISION_IDS=D1,D2,D3,D4,D5,D6`; `OWNER_DECISIONS_ACCEPTED=0`; `OWNER_DECISION_REGISTER_CONSISTENT=yes`; no D7; D1-D6 untouched in content, count and status; no implementation default selected.
- D5/D6 rationale preserved and not regressed: `D6_CLASSIFICATION_DEFENSIBLE=yes`; `D6_INDEPENDENCE_RATIONALE_CORRECTED=yes`; `D6_HARM_DOMINANCE_CLAIM_REMOVED=yes`; `D6_IS_ONLY_PATH_TO_PERMANENT_ACCOUNT_CLOSURE=no`; `D6_STRICTLY_GREATER_HARM_THAN_D5=no`; `D5_CAN_EVENTUALLY_LEAD_TO_ACTIVE_CLOSURE_ACKNOWLEDGED=yes`; `D5_D6_LOGICAL_INDEPENDENCE=PASS`; the §17.3 independence matrix is unchanged. §17.3's D5(b) composed-closure sentence gained only the qualifier that reactivation is a **nonterminal** outcome, which makes its replacement claim follow from the new rule instead of from the withdrawn unconditional one. §26.5 and §26.6 were not edited at all.
- Future validation updated in place, adding no test and rewriting none beyond the three W2 touches: test 12 now asserts the outcome-aware rule on both sides with replay failing closed in each; test 37 asserts mandatory confirmed replacement after a nonterminal reactivation and that recovery capability is not reported ready before confirmation; test 44 asserts that a successful terminal closure issues no replacement, records no outstanding obligation, creates no ordinary session and creates no new authentication or recovery authority, that the consumed credential stays unreplayable, and that a non-completing closure attempt leaves the nonterminal obligation intact. `FUTURE_AUTH_VALIDATION_PLAN_COMPLETE=yes`.
- Threat traceability: only THREAT-020 and THREAT-021 updated, for the credential-lifecycle half of the stale-authority and escalation invariants — no stale recovery authority survives a terminal transition, and a closure cannot mint authority a closed account is not entitled to hold. THREAT-022 deliberately untouched, because consumption-without-replacement creates no operator capability. No threat newly traced, none retired, none declared solved. `THREAT_TRACEABILITY_COMPLETE=yes`; `THREATS_DECLARED_SOLVED_BY_DESIGN=none`. - The gap, restated from its own wording: the outcome-aware §11.2 rule (W2's fix) was correct for a completed nonterminal or terminal outcome, but a disabled-account closure ceremony that verifies/uses the accepted recovery authority and then fails, is abandoned, or expires **before** `DISABLED` → `CLOSED` commits could be read as having already consumed the credential and created a replacement obligation, while defining no authority capable of satisfying that obligation from `DISABLED`.
- Repair: defined `RECOVERY_CREDENTIAL_CONSUMPTION_COMMIT_POINT=SUCCESSFUL_COMMIT_OF_THE_AUTHORIZED_RECOVERY_OR_ACCOUNT_STATE_TRANSITION` as a new §11.2 bullet placed before the existing single-use bullet. Presenting a credential, verifying it, and being admitted to a ceremony are each explicitly **not** consumption (`RECOVERY_CREDENTIAL_VERIFICATION_EQUALS_CONSUMPTION=no`, `CEREMONY_ENTRY_EQUALS_CONSUMPTION=no`); only a successful commit consumes it (`SUCCESSFUL_COMMIT_CONSUMES_CREDENTIAL=yes`). This does not loosen ceremony containment before that point — the ceremony remains exactly as narrowly authorized as §17.1 already states.
- Rewrote the §11.2 "only an actual terminal transition is terminal" bullet, which was the load-bearing defect: it previously said a failed/expired closure "falls under the nonterminal rule" and "if the credential was nonetheless consumed, the replacement obligation stands," which is exactly the ambiguity W3 flagged. It now states that a ceremony which does not commit has consumed **nothing at all** — the credential remains valid and no replacement obligation is created by that attempt (`FAILED_PRECOMMIT_CLOSURE_CONSUMES_RECOVERY_CREDENTIAL=no`, `STRANDED_DISABLED_CONSUMED_CREDENTIAL_STATE_ALLOWED=no`). The nonterminal/terminal split from revision 6 is unchanged in substance; it now applies only once a transition actually commits.
- Added, as future implementation requirements and not as anything designed here: atomic commit of the account-state transition and credential consumption as one logical unit (`CLOSURE_STATE_AND_CREDENTIAL_CONSUMPTION_ATOMIC=yes`); authoritative-state-governs semantics for a response lost after a successful commit, so a retry cannot roll back `CLOSED`, re-run the transition, or mint new authority (`POST_COMMIT_RESPONSE_FAILURE_DOES_NOT_ROLL_BACK_SECURITY_STATE=yes`, `CLOSURE_RETRY_AFTER_COMMIT_IS_NON_AUTHORIZING=yes`); and single-commit/no-concurrent-replay semantics for one single-use credential (`RECOVERY_CREDENTIAL_SINGLE_COMMIT_SEMANTICS=yes`, `CONCURRENT_SUCCESSFUL_REPLAY_ALLOWED=no`). No database, transaction mechanism, or storage engine is selected for any of these — only the invariant a future implementation must satisfy.
- §17.1's closure-ceremony and reactivation-ceremony `Failure` clauses were each extended with one sentence stating the credential/account consequence explicitly (remains valid / remains `DISABLED`, no replacement obligation), and the closure ceremony's `After success` clause's dangling "if the transition does not occur, §11.2's nonterminal rule governs instead" was corrected to point at the new non-commit rule instead, since nothing was consumed for the nonterminal rule to attach an obligation to. `FAILED_CLOSURE_RECOVERY_AUTHORITY_SAFE_STATE_DEFINED=yes`.
- **No new owner decision was created.** This is a security state-machine/failure-semantics precision fix — sharpening when "successful use" occurs — not a new product-policy option. `OWNER_DECISION_COUNT=6`; `OWNER_DECISION_IDS=D1,D2,D3,D4,D5,D6`; `OWNER_DECISIONS_ACCEPTED=0`; no D7; D1-D6, D5/D6 independence, and the D3/Gate 0C boundary untouched in content.

- Threat traceability: only THREAT-020 and THREAT-021 updated again, for the commit-point definition and pre-commit/post-commit failure semantics. THREAT-022 untouched, because a non-committing ceremony creates no operator capability either. No threat newly traced, none retired, none declared solved. `THREAT_TRACEABILITY_COMPLETE=yes`; `THREATS_DECLARED_SOLVED_BY_DESIGN=none`. Counterexample verified against the design as previously written: D5(b)+D6(b), closure attempt A and reactivation attempt B race; B commits `DISABLED`→`ACTIVE` and consumes the credential first; A's antecedent ("no other attempt committed `DISABLED`→`CLOSED`") was still true, so the old clause's `DISABLED`/valid consequent would have applied against an authoritative `ACTIVE`/consumed state. Both `Failure` clauses (closure, and symmetrically reactivation) now enumerate every competing transition away from `DISABLED` and add an explicit bullet for the cross-ceremony case: the loser must not restore `DISABLED`, must not restore credential validity, must not close or reactivate the account merely because it lost, and must not undo the winner's transition. `COMPETING_REACTIVATION_COVERED=yes`; `CONCURRENT_LOSER_ACCOUNT_STATE=AUTHORITATIVE_CURRENT_STATE`; `CONCURRENT_LOSER_CREDENTIAL_STATE=AUTHORITATIVE_CURRENT_STATE`; `CONCURRENT_LOSER_MAY_RESTORE_DISABLED=no`; `CONCURRENT_LOSER_MAY_RESTORE_CREDENTIAL_VALIDITY=no`; `CLOSURE_VS_REACTIVATION_RACE_DEFINED=yes`.
